8 
 2. 출자 단가와 실적

기준이 다른 
세 가치 지표 
 SK㈜ 반기보고서의 NPS 장부가와 지분율로 역산한 장부가 기준 NPS 가치는 약 16조
원이다. SK텔레콤의 취득 단가(642주, 3,971억원)에 신주 발행 전 주식 수(101,875주)를 
곱하면 약 63조원이다. 장부가 역산과 거래 단가는 산출 기준이 달라, 두 값의 차이만으
로 출자 가격의 적정성을 판단할 수는 없다. 낸드 사업 반기 순이익은 지난해 상반기 
0.13조원에서 올해 상반기 5.84조원으로 늘었다[그림9]. 보도된 상장 기업가치(약 136
조원)는 NPS가 아니라 솔리다임 기준 값이다[그림8]. 
출자 단가는 
계열사 공시로 확인 
 출자 단가는 신주를 받은 계열사의 공시와 주석에서 확인할 수 있다[표2]. SK텔레콤 약
정 잔여분(4년 분할)과 외부 프리IPO가 어떤 단가와 규모로 이뤄지는지가 상장 전 SK
하이닉스 주주의 몫을 정할 것이다.

[그림8] 기준이 다른 세 가치 지표(NPS·솔리다임) → 평가 대상·산출 방식이 달라 직접 비교에는 한계

주: 5~6월은 SK㈜ 별도 장부가(1,997억원) ÷ 지분율(1.27%)로 역산값. 세 값은 산출 기준이 달라 직접 비교에 한계. 
자료: DART, 블룸버그, 언론 보도, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[그림9] NPS 낸드 사업 반기 실적 → 매출 3.7배, 순이익 0.13조원에서 5.84조원으로

주: SK하이닉스 반기보고서 연결 주석의 NPS 요약재무 
자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

9 
 3. 솔리다임의 비중

기업가치로는 
시가총액의 약 13% 
10월 13일 종가 기준 
 NPS 낸드 사업이 SK하이닉스 연결에서 차지하는 몫은 상반기 매출의 9.3%, 순이익의 
4.3%, 자산의 5.7%, 순자산의 3.1%다. 순이익 비중이 낮은 것은 연결 순이익에 금융상
품 평가이익 등이 반영됐기 때문이다. 보도된 기업가치(약 136조원)는 10월 13일 SK하
이닉스 시가총액(1,054조원)의 약 13%다(회계 비중과는 기준이 다름)[그림10]. 
회사 입장은 
확정된 사항 없음

SK하이닉스는 8월 5일과 9월 4일 보도 관련 공시에서 솔리다임이 경쟁력 강화를 위해 
다양한 방안을 검토하고 있으나 확정된 사항은 없다고 밝혔다. 다음 재공시 예정일은 
12월 3일이다. SK텔레콤의 NPS 취득 공시와 SK하이닉스의 보도 관련 공시 원문은 [그
림11]에 실었다.

[그림10] SK하이닉스 안의 NPS(낸드) 비중 → 회계 숫자로는 3~9%, 보도된 기업가치로는 시가총액의 약 13%

주: NPS 낸드 사업 ÷ SK하이닉스 연결(2026년 6월 말). 연결 순이익에는 금융상품 평가이익 등 금융수익(상반기 82.9조원)이 반영. 기업가치 136조원은 1,000억달러 × 1,360원(SBS 
Biz, 2026.10.8 환산) 
자료: DART, KRX, 블룸버그, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[그림11] 공시 원문: SK텔레콤의 NPS 취득과 SK하이닉스의 보도 관련 공시 → 642주 3,971억원 취득 결정, 상장은 「확정된 사항 없음」

자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

10 
 4. 해외 상장에 적용되는 규율

8월부터 
엄격한 특례심사 
거래소 규정 · 8월 3일 
 금융위원회는 7월 31일 거래소 상장·공시 규정 개정을 의결해 8월 3일부터 중복상장을 
엄격한 특례심사 대상으로 바꿨다. 일반주주 권익을 고려하지 않는 비대칭적 중복상장
은 엄격히 금지한다는 취지다. 모회사 이사회는 독립 특별위원회 심의를 거쳐 주주영향
평가, 주주보호 방안, 주주 소통·동의 확인, 찬반 결의, 공시라는 다섯 가지 의무를 진다. 
금융위원회는 이 의무를 자회사 해외상장 때에도 적용한다고 밝혔다(금융위, 
2026.7.31)[그림12]. 
거래소 심사 미적용, 
당국은 규율 적용 입장 
 해외 상장은 거래소의 중복상장 특례심사를 거치지 않는다. 다만 금감원장은 금융위·금
감원 모두 중복상장에 해당한다고 본다고 답했다(10.19 국정감사). 주주동의가 필수인 
곳은 물적분할 자회사뿐이고, 솔리다임 같은 일반 자회사는 권고 대상이다(금융위, 
2026.7.31). 국회에는 자회사 상장 때 모회사 주총 특별결의를 받게 하는 자본시장법 개
정안(안도걸 의원 등 14인, 9월 9일 발의)이 정무위원회에 있다[표3].

[그림12] 금융위원회 중복상장 제도개선 구조도(원문) → 모회사 이사회 5 대 의무는 자회사 해외상장 때에도 적용

자료: 금융위원회, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[표3] 솔리다임 상장에 적용되는 규율 → 거래소 심사는 미적용이나 당국은 규율 적용 입장, 이사회 의무가 핵심 
규율 내용 솔리다임 적용 
공정거래법 제18조 지주회사는 자회사 외, 자회사는 손자회사 외 국내 계열회사 주식 소유 금지. 손자회사는 국내 계열회사 주식을 100% 가질 때만 소유 대상 아님(외국법인) 
거래소 중복상장 특례심사 자회사의 독립성, 이사회 5 대 의무 이행과 찬성 결의, 주주동의 여부 심사 미적용(미국 상장) 
모회사 이사회 5 대 의무 주주영향평가, 주주보호 방안, 주주 소통·동의 확인, 특별위원회 심의 뒤 찬반 결의, 단계별 공시 적용(금융위원회) 
주주동의 물적분할 자회사는 필수, 그 밖의 자회사는 권고. 3% 초과 의결권 제한, 출석 과반·전체 4분의 1 이상 권고(일반 자회사) 
자본시장법 개정안 자회사 상장 때 모회사 주총 특별결의(의안 2221240, 9월 9일 발의; SBS Biz, 2026.10.8) 정무위원회 계류

주: 중복상장 규정은 2026.7.31 의결, 8.3 시행. 해외 상장 적용은 금융위원회 보도참고자료(2026.7.31) 기준 
자료: 국가법령정보센터, 금융위원회, 국회 의안정보시스템, 언론 보도, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

### Page 11

ESG [한화리서치]

11 
 V. 주주가치: 공모 구조와 자금의 쓰임

1. 공모 구조별 SK 하이닉스 주주 손익

거래 직후 기준, 
신주든 구주든 가치 중립 
 공모가가 공정하면 신주 발행이든 구주매출이든 SK하이닉스 몫(지분가치와 현금의 합)
은 같다. 차이는 현금이 어디에 남느냐다. 신주를 내면 현금은 솔리다임에 남아 미국 공
장 등 투자 재원이 될 수 있고, 구주를 팔면 대금은 먼저 NPS에 들어오고, NPS의 배당
을 거쳐야 SK하이닉스의 환원 재원이 될 수 있다[표4]. 
전액 환원 가정 시 
주당 약 1만8,300원 
가정 · 100억달러 
 보도된 규모인 100억달러(약 13.6조원)를 구주매출로 조달하고, NPS가 이를 배당해 SK
하이닉스가 받은 현금(NPS 지분 98.16%분)을 다시 전액 특별배당한다고 가정하면 주
당 약 1만8,300원이다. 10월 13일 종가의 1.3%, 2025 사업연도 주당 배당(3,000원)의 6
배다. 다만 8월에 결정한 소각용 자사주 매입(40조원)과 견주면 3분의 1 규모다. 회사의 
배당 계획이 아니라 규모를 가늠하기 위한 가정 계산이다. 
추가 출자의 
가격과 조건 
 공모 전 외부 지분이 어떤 값과 조건으로 정해지는지도 SK하이닉스 주주의 몫을 좌우
한다. 출자 이후의 가치 상승분은 그 지분을 가진 계열사에 돌아간다. 다만 과거 출자 
단가가 상장 때의 기업가치보다 낮았다는 것만으로 기존 주주에게 불리한 거래였다고 
볼 수는 없다. 남은 출자와 프리IPO의 단가가 공정가에 가까울수록 SK하이닉스 주주의 
몫은 지켜진다. 
공모 구조의 평가, 
쓰임과 투자수익이 결정 
실적 개선 뒤의 값 
 보도된 기업가치(약 136조원)는 최근 실적 개선 뒤에 나온 값이다[그림8]. 이 값이 적정
한지는 이익이 이어지는지에 달려 있다. 구주매출은 판 지분만큼 지금의 평가를 현금으
로 바꾸는 길이고, 신주 발행은 지분을 일부 내주는 대신 미국 투자 재원을 솔리다임에 
넣어 수익으로 돌려받는 길이다. 신주로 조달한 자금을 미국 공장에 쓴다면, 주주가치는 
투자수익과 지정학적 위험을 줄이는 효과에 달려 있다. 어떤 공시가 나오면 무엇을 볼
지는 [표7]에 정리했다.

[표4] 공모 구조별 SK하이닉스 몫(가정) → 공정가면 지분가치와 현금의 합은 같고, 현금이 남는 곳만 다름 
구분 신주 발행 100% 구주매출 100% 
조달액(가정) 100억달러(약 13.6조원) 같음 
현금이 남는 곳 솔리다임(미국 공장 재원 가능) NPS·SK하이닉스(환원 재원 가능) 
SK하이닉스 실질 지분(6월 말 95.08%) 약 86.4% 약 85.3% 
SK하이닉스 몫(보도 기업가치 기준) 약 129조원 약 116조원 + 현금 13.3조원 
전액 환원 가정 시 주당 공모 현금은 솔리다임에 약 1만 8,300원(10/13 종가의 1.3%)

주: 1,000억달러를 공모 전 지분가치로 보고 100억달러를 조달한다고 둔 가정 계산. 거래 직후의 산술 비교로 주가 영향·투자수익·세금은 반영하지 않음. 당사 투자의견과 무관 
자료: DART, 블룸버그, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

### Page 12

ESG [한화리서치]

12 
 2. 종목별 영향과 판단 변수

종목마다 
받는 영향이 달라 
 솔리다임 상장이 SK그룹 상장사에 미치는 영향은 종목마다 다르다. SK하이닉스의 솔리
다임 실질 지분은 6월 말 공시 기준 95.08%로, 공모 구조·출자 가격·자금의 쓰임이 주
요 변수다. SK스퀘어는 SK하이닉스 지분을 통해 간접적으로 영향을 받는다. SK하이닉
스의 환원 정책과 보유 자산가치가 함께 반영된다. SK㈜·SK텔레콤·SK이노베이션은 
NPS 직접 지분의 가치 변화와 남은 출자 단가를 함께 봐야 한다[표5]. 
상장은 낸드 사업의 
가격표 
 솔리다임 상장은 SK하이닉스 연결 안에 있던 낸드 사업에 시장 가격을 따로 붙인다. 시
가총액의 약 13%로 보도된 기업가치가 독립된 값으로 평가받는 계기다[그림10]. 회사
는 8월에 40조원 규모의 소각용 자사주 매입을 결정해 환원 원칙을 이미 보여 줬다. 시
장이 확인하려는 것은 구주매출로 현금이 생길 때 이 원칙이 이어지는지다. 변동성이 
큰 시기에는 주주보호 방안을 먼저 공개할수록 시장의 신뢰를 빨리 얻을 수 있다. 
판단을 바꿀 
변수 네 가지 
 이 판단을 바꿀 변수는 네 가지다. 상장 시점은 메모리 업황과 미국 기술주 공모 시장의 
분위기에 달려 있다. 미국의 2027년분 연간 허가는 다롄 장비 반입을 정한다. 중국 당
국의 조건 해제는 중국 판매가와 생산 의무를 바꾼다. 미국 공장은 투자 규모와 보조금 
조건이 확정돼야 수익성을 따질 수 있다. 
해외 상장, 
다른 그룹의 선례 
 해외 상장에는 거래소 심사가 적용되지 않지만, 당국은 중복상장 규율을 적용한다는 입
장이다. 금융위원회의 유권해석은 해외 자회사 상장 전반의 선례가 된다. 현대차(2024
년 10월)와 LG전자(2025년 10월)는 인도 법인을 현지에 상장하며 보유 주식을 파는 
구주매출을 택했다. 국회 법안이 대통령령으로 해외 증시까지 넓히면 모회사 주총 특별
결의가 해외 상장에도 걸린다(SBS Biz, 2026.10.8). 해외 자회사 상장을 검토하는 그룹은 
이 법안의 진행을 함께 봐야 한다.

[표5] SK그룹 상장사별 솔리다임 노출과 판단 조건 → 같은 상장이라도 종목마다 봐야 할 조건이 다름 
종목 지분 보유 경로 영향 경로 주요 판단 조건 
SK하이닉스 NPS 98.16% × 솔리다임 96.86%(실질 95.08%) 신주면 지분 희석·현금은 솔리다임에, 구주매출이면 NPS에 현금 유입. 주가는 메모리 업황이 주변수 공모가의 적정성, 신주·구주 비율, 조달 자금의 투자수익과 환원, 남은 출자 단가 
SK스퀘어 SK하이닉스 지분 20.0%를 거친 간접 노출 보유 자산가치와 SK하이닉스 환원 정책이 함께 반영 SK하이닉스의 환원 정책, 순자산가치 대비 할인율  
SK㈜ NPS 직접 1.27%(6월 말), SK스퀘어·SK하이닉스를 통한 간접 보유 직접 지분의 경제적 가치가 SK㈜에 귀속 추가 출자의 단가·권리조건과 희석 영향, 총약정 2.5억달러 중 남은 납입액 
SK텔레콤 NPS 0.62%(642주, 취득 결정 기준) 직접 지분의 가치 변화, 4년 분할 출자 추가 출자의 단가·권리조건과 희석 영향, 총약정 4.8억달러 중 남은 납입액 
SK이노베이션 미국 법인 경유 NPS 출자(약정 3.8억달러) 미국 법인이 보유한 NPS 지분의 가치 변화와 추가 출자 부담 추가 출자의 단가·권리조건과 희석 영향,  잔여 약정의 납입 부담과 출자 재원

주: 지분은 2026년 6월 말 공시 기준(SK스퀘어는 7월 ADR 뒤, SK텔레콤은 취득 결정 기준). 노출 경로 정리로 투자의견·순위 아님 
자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

### Page 13

ESG [한화리서치]

13 
 3. 확인할 공시와 결론

상장 사슬이 
네 단계로 
 SK㈜·SK스퀘어·SK하이닉스는 이미 모두 상장돼 있다. 솔리다임까지 상장하면 SK㈜에
서 솔리다임으로 이어지는 수직 사슬에 상장사가 네 곳 놓인다. 상장사가 늘어날수록 
각 단계의 주주에게 가치와 현금이 어떻게 전달되는지를 설명하는 일이 중요해진다. 
보호 방안의 
내용과 이행 
 가이드라인은 주주보호 방안의 예로 자회사 주식 현물배당과 자사주 소각을 든다. SK하
이닉스 이사회가 주주영향평가와 보호 방안을 언제, 어떤 내용으로 공시하는지가 확인
할 첫 문서다. 미국 공장의 필요성과 자회사 상장의 필요성은 따로 따질 문제다. 이사회
가 설명할 것은 다른 조달 수단과 견줘 상장이 기존 주주에게 유리한지, 그 근거는 무엇
인가다. 다음 재공시 예정일(12월 3일)을 포함해 확인할 일정은 [표6]에 정리했다. 
결론: 상장 기대보다 
조건 확인이 먼저

SK하이닉스 주가의 큰 방향은 여전히 메모리 업황이 정한다. 솔리다임 상장은 SK하이
닉스 안의 낸드 사업이 어떻게 평가받는지를 정하는 변수다. 그 평가를 가르는 조건은 
세 가지다. 상장 전 출자 단가가 적정한지, 공모 자금이 미국 투자와 모회사 가운데 어
디로 가는지, 투자수익이나 환원 계획이 구체적인지다. SK㈜·SK텔레콤·SK이노베이션은 
NPS 지분의 가치와 남은 출자 부담을 함께 봐야 한다. 상장과 미국 공장은 아직 확정
되지 않았다. 지금은 상장 기대를 미리 더하기보다, 금융위원회의 유권해석과 이사회의 
주주보호 방안, 12월 3일 재공시에서 이 조건을 확인할 때다.

[표6] 확인할 공시와 일정 → 공모가보다 상장 전 단가와 공모 자금의 쓰임이 먼저 
시점 확인할 것 
10월 28일 SK하이닉스 3 분기 실적발표(컨퍼런스콜, 10월 14일 안내공시): 솔리다임·NPS 관련 질의 여부 
11월 19일 SK하이닉스 소각 목적 자사주 매입(40조원, 8월 19일 결정)의 매입 기간 종료: 실제 취득 규모와 소각 이행 
12월 3 일 SK하이닉스 솔리다임 관련 보도 관련 공시의 재공시 예정일(9월 4 일 공시) 
12월 중국 인수 조건 5 년 경과: 해제 신청 여부와 당국 결정. 미국 2027년분 장비 반입 허가 여부(2026년분은 지난해 12월 발급) 
수시 SK하이닉스 이사회 특별위원회 구성, 주주영향평가, 주주보호 방안(자회사 주식 현물배당·자사주 소각 등) 공시 
수시 NPS 추가 신주 발행 단가: SK텔레콤 약정 잔여분(4년 분할), 외부 프리IPO 투자(보도) 
미정 금융위원회 유권해석: 솔리다임 상장의 국내 증권신고서 제출 대상 여부 
미정 솔리다임 미국 공장 부지·투자 규모 발표 
2027년(Bloomberg) 솔리다임 뉴욕 상장: 신주와 구주매출의 비율, 공모 자금의 쓰임, 상장 뒤 SK하이닉스 지분율

주: 예정 일정은 바뀔 수 있음 
자료: DART, 국회 의안정보시스템, 언론 보도, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[표7] 공시별 판단 기준 → 상장 여부보다 어떤 공시가 나오느냐가 판단을 바꿈 
공시로 확인되는 것 주주가치 판단의 초점 함께 볼 증거 
신주 중심 공모 지분율 하락보다 조달 자금의 투자수익 투자 규모·집행 일정·보조금 조건 
구주매출, 대금이 NPS에 남음 매각 가격과 현금의 재투자 계획 자금 사용 계획·세금·비용 
구주매출, 모회사 환원 구체화 SK하이닉스로 오는 현금과 환원 규모 배당·소각 결의와 집행 일정 
계열사 추가 출자·프리IPO 거래 당시의 가격·규모와 조건 외부 투자자 가격·주식 권리 
상장 지연·철회 기존 낸드 사업가치의 지속성 실적·현금흐름·대체 자금 조달

주: 판단 기준의 정리로 전망·투자의견 아님 
자료: 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026년 10월 20일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성됐음을 확인합니다. 당사는 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현
재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 당사는 이 자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (박세연) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 이 자료는 특정 종목의 매수·매도를 권유하지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터
가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 
책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사용될 수 없습니다.

Document 269: De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-09-15
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
De-reservification, Not De-dollarization
The world isn’t moving away from the dollar. It is shifting its dollars from traditional
reserves to state banks, pension funds, and other quasi-sovereign investors.
(Bernardo Montoya/Courtesy Reuters)
PUBLISHED
September 15, 2026 8:37 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
This is a joint blog with Alex Etra, Senior Macro
Strategist and head of Flow of Funds at Vanda
Research.
The IMF’s data on the currency composition of
foreign exchange (FX) reserves is scrutinized carefully
for any signs that the world is shifting way from the
dollar.
Yet it doesn’t really matter that much if the dollar’s
share of reserves has shifted from 56.5 percent to 57
percent. The ink-to-impact ratio of the quarterly

reporting on the latest COFER data is all off.
Neither the stock of global reserves nor the stock of
dollar reserves has changed much in the last ten years.
The action is elsewhere.

### Page 3

3
China’s state banks (per the Bank of International
Settlements) have almost as many foreign assets as the
central bank (PBOC).
Japan’s Government Pension Investment Fund
(GPIF) has almost as many foreign assets ($986
billion) as the government has FX reserves ($1.1
trillion at the end of August). Japan’s FX reserves are
on the books of the Ministry of Finance and the
GPIF is overseen by the Ministry of Health, Welfare
and Labor—so these are almost all assets of the

Government of Japan or the broader public sector,
not its central bank.
Korea’s National Pension Service has more foreign
assets than the Bank of Korea has FX reserves.
A different dynamic is in play in Taiwan, as the
government doesn’t run a big retirement fund. But in

### Page 4

4
a world where the hedge ratio of the life insurers has
been (and still is) used as a policy tool, the shift in
Taiwan’s foreign asset accumulation away from the
Central Bank of China (CBC) and over to
institutions whose activities in the foreign currency
market it directly influences broadly fits into the same
theme. So too does the PBOC’s use of the
“macroprudential adjustment factor” effectively adjust
banks’ net foreign assets in much the same way that

CBC regulations adjust lifer hedge ratios.
In the last year, the lifers’ hedge ratios—and their
direct hedges with the central bank—have been one
of the key tools the CBC has used to manage the
Taiwan dollar (and guard the lifers’ solvency).
Asia’s big surplus economies are in a sense just
catching up with the world’s oil exporters. Norway
put its oil surplus into a sovereign wealth fund from
the start. Kuwait, Qatar, and Abu Dhabi (the most
oil-rich emirate) all also have large sovereign funds.

And the world’s biggest oil exporter has joined them:
The Public Investment Fund has almost as many
foreign assets as the Saudi Central Bank (SAMA).*
What’s more, the available evidence suggests that the
dollar share of these pools is in line with or higher
than the dollar share of global FX reserves (it depends
on the institution and jurisdiction).

### Page 5

5
In other words, by looking at the (easily available)
data on the world’s static holdings of formal FX
reserve assets, scholars and analysts miss most of the
growth in the world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign
foreign assets.
Consider China.
No serious analyst now disputes that China’s state
banks—including the policy banks (the China
Development Bank, China Exim)—hold several
trillion in foreign assets.
China reports $3.3 trillion in gross foreign assets to

the BIS (the net position, counting foreign bank
claims on the entire Chinese economy not just the
banks, is $2.5 trillion). That maps to the BOP data,
which shows almost $4 trillion in gross outflows
through the banking system (technically, the sum of
gross outflows in “other” plus the $500 billion in
foreign currency bonds held by the state commercial
banks)

### Page 6

6
Since 2010 (and even more so after 2014) the net
outflow through the state banks has exceeded reserve
accumulation.
The broad contours of this story are confirmed by the
balance sheet data reported by state commercial
banks in their 2025 annual reports, which showed

### Page 7

7
that the top five banks held a combined $2.5 trillion
in foreign currency assets (mostly held abroad).
The Chinese haven’t disclosed the foreign assets of
the two policy banks (with at this stage the complicity
of the IMF, which has neither analyzed the role of
SAFE policy bank financing, nor highlighted the
glaring gap in China’s own reporting). But the work
of AidData points to nearly $1 trillion in foreign
assets, with a hefty dollar share.
The available data sources all suggest that the bulk of

banks—puts the dollar share of their offshore foreign
currency assets at around 70 percent. That is well
above the last disclosed dollar share of China’s formal
reserves (55 percent in 2019). The banks’ annual

### Page 8

8
reports suggest that about 70 percent of the banks’
assets are in dollars and Hong Kong dollars (55
percent USD/15 percent HKD)—a bit below the
dollar and Hong Kong dollar share of their liabilities
(swaps out of the HKD fund a decent share of the
banks euro assets).
Put simply, SAFE’s static dollar holdings aren’t the
important story.
The real story is the rapid growth of the state banks
dollar holdings.
Japan holds a high (though undisclosed) dollar share

in its FX reserves, and those reserves are primarily
invested in Treasuries—so Japan’s MoF is now clearly
the largest contributor to the U.S. data on foreign
official holdings of Treasuries. (SAFE has shifted its
funds out of U.S. custodians, and thus increasingly
appears in the data as a “private” holder in a European
custodial center)
Japan’s formal reserves haven’t changed much in the
last ten years; periodic sales to limit the yen’s weakness
have offset most of the MoF’s accumulated interest

income. Foreign currency reserves were $1.2 trillion
in 2014 and $1.16 trillion at the end of Q1. They
dipped to about $1 trillion at the end of August after
sales of close to $100 billion at the end of July/start of
August.

### Page 9

9
What has grown is the foreign portfolio of the GPIF.
it was around $400 billion in 2014, ~$1 trillion in Q1
of 2026.
GPIF’s foreign bonds aren’t held exclusively in dollars,
but roughly 52 percent of its foreign bonds (26
percent of all bonds) are USD-denominated.
But the GPIF, like most international investors, holds
a large share of its equity portfolio in the U.S. (~$165
billion, 65 percent of the foreign equity portfolio).
Korea is a more extreme version of Japan.

The Bank of Korea’s FX reserves have been roughly
$375-450 billion over the last ten years or so. Around
two-thirds of those are now in dollars (as of the end of
2025).

### Page 10

10
From 2012 to 2025, the foreign assets at the NPS
have grown from ~$80 billion (around 21 percent of
total assets) to over $660 billion (55 percent of total
assets)—and something like 70 percent of those are in
dollars. 76 percent of the fund’s equities are in dollars,
and 66 percent of its debt. And while a lower share of
its “alternative” portfolio is in North America, most
alternative mandates are awarded in dollars.

### Page 11

11
In other words, the majority of the dollar assets held
by Korea’s government (the NPS reports to the
Ministry of Health and Welfare, and the Minister of
Health chairs its asset allocation committee) aren’t
held by the central bank and aren’t in the IMF’s
COFER data set.
Taiwan is an unusual case, as the life insurers aren’t
technically sovereign investors. But with their
regulator now explicitly encouraging the them to hold
an unhedged foreign currency book and hence they

now rely on the state for their solvency, they have a bit
of an official character (a significant appreciation of
the TWD would blow through their volatility reserves
and leave them with fewer assets than liabilities, as
their foreign assets are largely held against TWD
policies). The CBC certainly now uses the lifers desire
to unwind existing hedges to help manage the Taiwan

### Page 12

12
dollar. And they have an exceptionally high dollar
share in their foreign assets—over 95 percent.
The Saudi Public Investment Fund (PIF) is another
example.
It keeps roughly $200 billion of its $250 billion in
foreign investments in dollars—well above the U.S.
share of a standard global stock index. Almost all of
the PIF’s $30 billion in external debt is also in dollars.
And the PIF now has only slightly fewer foreign
assets than the Saudi Central Bank has reserves.**

The broad story is thus pretty clear: the growth in the
world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign assets is not
coming through an increase in FX reserve holdings
managed by the world’s central banks.

### Page 13

13
And, in the critical case of China, the dollar share of
China’s quasi-sovereign foreign assets is likely higher
than the dollar share of their formal FX reserves.
Adam Tooze has noted that inflows into the U.S. are
now coming from investors looking for a profit, not
investors looking for safety. What he calls the “profit
dollar” manifests itself in a number of different ways.
More inflows into corporate bonds and equities for
one. Less official demand for Treasuries for another.

More opacity too.
Those same trends are at play among sovereign and
quasi-sovereign investors.
So don’t obsess about the dollar’s chare in formal FX
reserves. Do recognize that the dollar’s “reserve
currency role” isn’t the source of any significant new
inflows into the dollar.

### Page 14

14
The dollar’s dominant role in the international
monetary system depends on much more than the
size and composition of central bank FX reserves. It is
as much, perhaps more so (given that FX reserve
managers are themselves ultimately liability matchers)
a function of the portfolio choice of a set of
private/semi-private/quasi-sovereign investors that
are much more difficult to observe. Reserve currency
status is not only about FX reserves in a strict sense.

That’s been true for some time. Most of the current
flow into U.S. from the “official” sector is coming
from investors who are not classic reserve managers.
Those flows remain heavily tilted toward the dollar, at
least for now.***
And any real de-dollarization would likely occur first
among these investors.
Put differently, the dollar’s global role is increasingly
as a source of returns, not a source of safety. The
foreign bid, private and public, is for risk, not for

Treasuries. That doesn’t help Scott Bessent much
right now, but it has helped keep valuations in the
stock market extended. And the global debate on the
dollar’s role lags the evolution in the dollar’s role, and
the risks associated with that new role.
It is currently fashionable in some circles to point to
the diminution of the “convenience yield” on

### Page 15

15
Treasuries even while the U.S. dollar continues to
enjoy such a privilege. But the shift in the global
investor base, and their apparent portfolio preference
for U.S. risk assets in lieu of Treasuries, may help
explain the divergence between the convenience yield
on UST and the convenience yield on USD.
And of course, if the negative convenience yield
implies that Treasuries are trading at a historical
discount, the persistence of the latter—and its

concentration in risk assets—suggests that the “profit
dollar” itself is likely trading at a historical premium.
That apparent premium on U.S. risk assets deserves as
much (or more) scrutiny as the currency composition
of central bank FX reserves.
* The PIF and Saudi Aramco also now have a certain
amount of debt.
** Reserves (now mostly in Treasuries, so should
appear in the TIC data, unlike at some times in the
past.

### Page 16

16
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

CAPITAL FLOWS BANKING
*** Japan is the main exception; the GPIF’s dollar
share is below the likely dollar share of Japan’s MoF.

### Page 2

ESG与可持续发展周报
3 方面考虑。一是能源革命是新一轮科技革命和产业变革的关键赛
道。当前，以绿色低碳为标志的能源革命正在全面跨过技术积累拐
点，进入到快速商业化规模化发展阶段。国际可再生能源署数据显
示，2010—2025 年，光伏、陆上风电度电成本分别累计下降 89%、
71%；2025 年全球超九成新建可再生能源发电项目成本低于化石能源
电站。国际能源署预计，2026 年全球能源总投资将达到 3.4 万亿美
元，其中 2.2 万亿美元投向风电、光伏、储能、氢能等清洁能源领
域，已成为全球布局新一轮科技革命和产业变革的重要方向。二是积
极推进能源革命是实现碳达峰碳中和的必由之路。从排放源头看，能
源活动贡献了我国 80%以上的碳排放量，其中电力行业碳排放超过全
社会碳排放的 40%，实现碳达峰碳中和目标的主要矛盾集中在以化石
能源为主的能源结构上。中国式现代化以实体经济为根基，能源的饭
碗必须端在自己手里。中长期看，我国能源刚性需求仍将继续增长。
从源头上减碳是实现碳达峰碳中和的治本之策，要求我们必须通过清

洁能源规模化扩张，覆盖新增用能需求，减少化石能源消费存量。三
是发展新质生产力需要通过能源革命来提供可靠安全保障。当前，地
区冲突频发加剧地缘政治风险，能源问题政治化、武器化属性更加突
出，全球能源市场波动加剧。发展新质生产力，建设现代化产业体
系，特别是满足全国一体化算力网、新一代通信网等新型基础设施建
设，以及集成电路、人工智能、新能源汽车等新兴产业用能增长需
求，推动产业高端化、智能化、绿色化，要求我们积极推动能源革
命，持续提高国内能源供给占比。
《意见》围绕着力推进发展方式创新，部署构建绿色低碳循环发
展的经济体系、优化绿色发展政策工具箱和积极推进能源革命三项举
措。我们认为，新质生产力推动可持续发展，我国正迈入以新质生产
力为核心驱动的绿色发展新阶段。
 行业动态
（1）近年来，我国保险业在面对重大自然灾害时，响应速度与理赔力
度不断提升，社会保障功能持续显现。今年 5月以来，受强降雨、台
风等影响，广西、湖北、甘肃等地接连发生城市内涝、水库溃坝、山
体滑坡等灾情。中国保险行业协会数据显示，截至 2026年 7月末，面

对 20 个省份的暴雨、台风等灾害，保险业已快速赔付 55.3 亿元。
2025 年全年，保险业针对重大自然灾害的赔付高达约 346.72 亿元。
当前我国保险赔付占自然灾害直接经济损失的比重仅约 10%，仍有较
大保障缺口。巨灾往往具有低频高损、破坏力极强的特点，仅靠事后
被动补偿，社会整体损失依然沉重。因此，破局的关键在于前置——
推动保险服务从出险以后赔向灾害来临前防延伸。前置风险防线，重
点在科技预警和风险减量。保险机构应把服务链条前移，建立覆盖多
灾种的监测预警和应急响应机制，运用气象监测、遥感、地理信息、
物联网等技术，对重点区域、重点标的进行动态跟踪，在灾害来临前
向客户精准预警，协助做好人员转移、设施加固、农作物抢收等防护
工作。将防灾减损费用纳入产品设计，与应急管理、气象、水利等部
门建立信息共享和联动机制，推动灾前预防、灾中干预、灾后理赔有
效衔接，真正降低灾害损失。
（2）10月 14日，全国首批绿证跨境集中交易（香港）发布活动在广
州举行，本次交易合计成交约 10万个国家可再生能源绿色电力证书，

对应可再生能源电量近 1亿千瓦时。本次交易实现内地绿证规模化进
入香港市场，搭建起内地绿证市场对接香港国际绿色金融市场的流通
渠道，是粤港澳大湾区推进绿色能源协同创新的标志性实践。

### Page 3

ESG与可持续发展周报
 技术前沿
10 月 15 日，中国石油塔里木 120 万吨乙烯工程商业运营新闻发
布会在北京举行，项目即日起全面投入商业运营。标志着中国石油在
新疆形成 300万吨级乙烯产业长周期稳定运营能力。作为我国首个全
链条绿色低碳乙烯工程、中西部单体规模最大乙烯项目，工程总投资
256.56 亿元，整体国产化率 99%。可年产 90 万吨全密度聚乙烯、45
万吨聚丙烯、10万吨顺丁橡胶，橡塑产品总产能达 145万吨，填补南
疆高端聚烯烃、合成橡胶新材料产业空白，带动区域下游形成千亿级
产业集群。
自今年 7月 16 日一次开车成功以来，工程已累计生产乙烯超 26
万吨，外供优质化工品 56.6 万吨，各类产品合格率达 100%，聚丙
烯、MTBE（甲基叔丁基醚）产品远销中亚市场。除此之外，工程搭
建“绿电直连+碳捕集+蓝氢+蓝氨+绿色化肥”全链条低碳技术闭环，
建成国内首套自主技术乙烯三机电驱装置和全球首个乙烯裂解炉烟气
碳循环利用示范基地。通过回收乙烯裂解炉烟气中的二氧化碳生产尿

素，实现“以废制肥”。目前，工程已捕集利用二氧化碳超 10万吨，
产出绿色尿素超 12万吨。工程可年减碳 137万吨，固碳生态效益相当
于新增 548万亩森林碳汇。
 研究探索
近日，由中国经济信息社主办的“蓄势与新篇：2026耐心资本与
可持续发展大会”在湖南长沙举行。在大会上，中国金融学会绿色金
融专业委员会主任、北京绿色金融与可持续发展研究院院长马骏表
示，我国逐步形成了以界定标准、信息披露、激励机制、产品体系为
四大支柱的绿色金融体系，绿色金融规模持续扩大。马骏指出，钢
铁、水泥、有色、化工等高碳行业低碳转型的融资需求，可能超过光
伏、风电等纯绿活动。应借助绿色科技提升转型项目的经济效益，识
别兼具减碳效益和经济性的技术路径。由其牵头筹建、获香港特区政
府支持的“绿色加速器”公益平台已经启动，将支持我国绿色科技企
业在“一带一路”沿线落地可融资项目，助力绿色企业出海。中国能
源研究会能源金融与法律分会主任委员陈景东在主旨演讲“绿色金
融——政策与实践”中表示，绿色财税和绿色金融为能源绿色低碳转

型发挥了重要作用。随着新能源行业不断发展，财税政策正在逐步调
整完善，绿色金融的支持政策和实践将更多来自资本市场。在绿色转
型过程中，能源企业需要更多权益类资金，希望财税和金融部门继续
加大对能源行业转型的支持。
 国际合作
10 月 20-22 日，“2026 可持续全球领导者大会”将在黄浦滨江
拉开帷幕。大会将汇聚来自全球的 300余位顶级嘉宾，包括联合国相
关机构、多个国际组织的代表、诺贝尔奖与图灵奖得主、国内外领军
企业高管及知名智库专家，围绕全球可持续发展的现实挑战与机遇开
展高水平对话。本届大会共有 22 个分论坛，议题涵盖：AI 发展与人
性未来，气候治理与生态保护，清洁能源与安全保障，绿色消费与品
牌使命，教育转型与科技赋能等。
 投资建议
《关于发展新质生产力的意见》围绕着力推进发展方式创新，部
署构建绿色低碳循环发展的经济体系、优化绿色发展政策工具箱和积
极推进能源革命三项举措。我们认为，新质生产力推动可持续发展，

September 2026: Marked improvement in

to a 3-year high
Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas
ECONOMIC RESEARCH
Change since m = 0 = February 2026 / February 2022.

Document 304: GF - PTFE Adoption

Source PDF: all report pdf/GF - PTFE Adoption.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Equity Research 
Sector Report | Overseas Technology｜Oct 15,2026 
PTFE Adoption: 
Reshape the High-End Materials Landscape

Overseas  
Technology

Michelle Jing 
SFC CE No. BUK594 
michellejing@gfgroup.com.hk 
Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk

What's new 
Nvidia showcased its NVLink PTFE board prototype for the first time at OCP Global Summit 2026, highlighting seven supply-chain partners: 
Taiflex (8039 TT), Flexium (6269 TT), TTM Technologies (TTMI US), 3 leading Chinese PCB manufacturers, and 1 leading Chinese CCL 
supplier. We believe this development reinforces our previous view that PTFE remains the most promising material solution for the VR300 
NVL576 switch board, offering the best balance between electrical performance and MP feasibility. 
Comments 
PTFE hybrid remains the preferred solution: As highlighted in our Oct. 5 report, we continue to favor the PTFE hybrid solution for the 
VR300 NVL576 switch board among the various options, given its optimal balance between electrical performance and MP feasibility. 
While pure PTFE remains challenging to process, particularly in lamination and drilling, leading PCB manufacturers have achie ved 
encouraging yields with hybrid structures that combine PTFE signal layers with M9 materials in non-signal layers. 
We like PTFE's long-term adoption potential: PTFE applications in high-frequency and high-speed interconnects could extend beyond 
switch boards. The IEC has published a standard on PTFE-filled CCL, led by the leading Chinese CCL company, highlighting the material's 
relevance to broader circuit-board applications. Our checks also suggest that PTFE materials have entered the testing phase for other 
applications, including compute boards. We therefore remain positive on PTFE's long-term adoption across a broader range of high-end 
PCB applications. 
Limited near-term impact on conventional resin materials, but long-term substitution risks warrant attention: Under the current 
PTFE hybrid design, we expect 8–12 out of 32 layers to adopt PTFE for signal transmission, accounting for less than 50% of the total CCL 
resin content value. Over the longer term, we expect a potential shift toward pure PTFE solutions as improvements in processing 
technology enable commercially viable MP yields, reducing the need for hybrid structures. This could gradually increase substitution risks 
for conventional high-end PCB resin materials, including PPO and HC resin. 
Favor PTFE-exposed suppliers with proven technical and manufacturing capabilities: We remain positive on PTFE-related 
opportunities, particularly suppliers with strong R&D capabilities, proven MP expertise, and established customer qualifications. In CCL, 
we favor Taiflex (8039 TT) and leading Chinese CCL supplier for their PTFE material capabilities and supply-chain exposure. Among the 
five Nvidia-announced PCB suppliers, we favor the leading Chinese PTFE PCB supplier, given its advanced hybrid PTFE processing 
capabilities and encouraging yields. For CCL suppliers that have yet to enter the PTFE supply chain, we recommend monitoring progress 
in developing, while remaining alert to potential market-share losses in their conventional product portfolios. 
Risks  
1) AI demand slowing down; 2) Geo-political risks; 3) Competition.

Document 314: Insurance costs hit the brakes Insurance payment growth is cooling, but the relief consumers are seeing depends heavily on who and where they are. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Insurance costs hit the brakes Insurance payment growth is cooling, but the relief consumers are seeing depends heavily on who and where they are. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13028742     1

Economy 
Insurance costs hit the brakes 
16 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Property insurance (e.g., auto, homeowners, renters) payment growth has slowed considerably, according to Bank of America 
payments data. Median payments rose just 1% year-over-year (YoY) in 2026, easing further from the 6% growth seen in 2025, 
after a roughly 12% increase in 2024, suggesting that many consumers are getting some relief from rising insurance payments. 
• However, the moderation is uneven across consumers. Bank of America payments data suggests that Gen Z continues to 
experience insurance payment growth of nearly 6%, while increases for other generations are closer to 1% to 2%. Payments 
also rose nearly 1.5% for higher-income households but edged lower for those with lower incomes. 
• Lower accident rates, easing vehicle prices, and softer major-purchase growth may be reducing cost pressures, but geography 
remains a major dividing line. Florida metros recorded declines of roughly 7% to 8%, while California and several Northeastern 
metros continued to see relatively strong increases.

Insurance payments stabilize after years of painful growth 
The burden of higher property (e.g., auto, renters, homeowners) insurance payments may be easing for some. Notably, while the 
average payment increased 3% year-over-year (YoY), the median payment was only up 1%, according to Bank of America 
payments data. This suggests that most consumers have only seen a small increase in their premiums, though there may be a 
few outliers (Exhibit 1).  
The latest data stands in contrast to just two years ago in 2024, when most consumers saw their insurance payments surge 
about 12% YoY, after a nearly 8% YoY increase in 2023.   
Exhibit 1: Insurance payments have increased only modestly through August of this year, with growth peaking in 2024 
Median and average insurance payments (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Insurance payments by age show a similar pattern, with much stronger growth in 2024 across most generations followed by a 
cooling since then (Exhibit 2). Gen Z is the outlier here, with their payments still growing at a healthy rate, increasing almost 6% 
YoY for both 2026 and 2025, after a 7% increase in 2024. Meanwhile, Millennials and Baby Boomers experienced a 2% YoY 
increase so far this year, with Gen X only seeing around a 1% rise.

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Bank of America payments data by income provides even more insight. Higher-income households paid nearly 1.5% more in 
2026 than they did a year ago, while middle-income households saw only modest increases or none at all (Exhibit 3). Conversely, 
lower-income households actually paid slightly less so far this year than they did last year.  
Exhibit 3: Higher-income households are still paying more for insurance compared to last year, while lower earners are paying less 
Median insurance payments by income cuts (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Fewer accidents and lower vehicles prices may be driving insurance payments down 
But why might insurance payments be declining at all? One thing to keep in mind is that insurance companies often raise rates 
to cover events that occurred in previous years to recover costs, while adjusting for changing risks.  
For example, the crash rate – the number of vehicle crashes per one million miles driven – suggests that there was a sharp rise 
in accidents in 2021, according to the Department of Transportation (Exhibit 4). Additionally, new and used vehicle prices 
increased significantly after the pandemic, according to Consumer Price Index (CPI) data from the Bureau of Labor Statistics 
(BLS) (Exhibit 5). So, insurers were not only facing more vehicle replacements due to a higher crash rate, but a higher cost to 
replace vehicles as well.

However, the crash rate mostly declined from 2021 to 2024, and the Department of Transportation reported that the number of 
accidents likely decreased again in 2025. Also, while the cost to maintain and repair vehicles has consistently grown since 2021, 
the price to replace vehicles peaked in 2022 and has eased since then – the latter likely a more important factor in insurance 
costs than the former. Additionally, while the CPI index for motor vehicle insurance has fallen 5% YoY, it’s possible that this may 
partially be due to rebates, according to BofA Global Research. So, the price figured into CPI may not be fully realized by the 
consumer.

Another factor in the divergence between a declining CPI and rising insurance payments in our data may be the growth in the 
amount of property people are insuring now. Notably though, this growth has eased in recent years. YoY growth in both new 
home and total vehicle sales peaked in mid-2023 and has grown modestly, or even declined, since (Exhibit 6). And given that 
these major purchases are still K-shaped (read more in Autos: In gear, but the “K” is still in the headlights and On the move: 
Renovation over relocation?), it could help to explain the divergence in insurance payment growth across income cohorts.  
Exhibit 6: Growth in new home and total vehicle sales peaked in mid-2023 due to a post-pandemic boom but has eased since

  I NSTI TUTE

Florida’s metros have seen the largest decline when compared to other major cities 
Examining different cities across the US also sheds light on insurance trends. Consumers in major California metros saw the 
largest increases in 2026, following near double digit growth in 2025, likely driven by widespread property damage from 
wildfires, according to BofA Global Research (Exhibit 7). Major coastal metros in the Northeast like Boston and New York also 
experienced sizable increases as well.  
Conversely, many cities throughout the Sun Belt like Houston, Nashville and Dallas saw flat or even declining insurance 
payments. BofA Global Research points to several reasons for these declines: part of the equation in this region is that the 
massive runup in the cost of property insurance and domestic migration to the region through 2024 has since eased or reversed 
course.   
Exhibit 7: California major metros have seen steady insurance payment growth, while Miami metros have seen declines 
Median insurance payments by core based statistical areas (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
In Florida, legislation that went into effect in 2023, eventually made its way into insurance prices in 2025, leading to widespread 
declines in premiums. The state also has a “lookback period” of three years, where insurance profits can be deemed too high and 
a rebate is issued to consumers. It’s possible this rebate is factoring into declines this year, as some carriers only allow rebates 
upon renewals.  
The Sunshine State was a notable standout for declines, with Orlando, Tampa and Jacksonville each down around 8% YoY in 
2026, followed closely by Miami with a 7% decrease. Additionally, Bank of America payments data suggests that Jacksonville, 
Miami and Orlando all had median insurance payments above the national level in 2023, though their payments were fell below 
that in 2026 (Exhibit 8).  
Major metros in Texas also saw easing insurance payments relative to 2023, while Seattle, San Jose, and Riverside, CA are 
among the major metros that went from below the national median to above in the three-year period ending in 2026.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Insurance payments include renters, auto or home, but do not include insurance escrowed into mortgage payments or deducted from a 
paycheck.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Document 351: Preview of RBNZ October 2026 Monetary Policy Review (2026-10-01)

### Page 1

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Following on?
• We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps
to 3% at the 28 October Review.
• A vote seems very possible as differences in
view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook
has really shifted in recent weeks.
• Data since September show a higher outlook
for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP
starting point, but increased risks to domestic
consumption and residential investment. On

balance, the medium-term inflation picture
will probably be judged to have worsened
slightly given recent movements in energy
prices and the exchange rate.
• Forward guidance will likely leave open
the option to raise the OCR further at the
December Monetary Policy Statement without
presenting it as a sure thing.
• Given the ‘pause’ signal in September, the
press release will likely emphasise the more
concerning short-term inflation picture driven

by the lower exchange rate and higher energy
prices. The tightening in financial conditions
through higher short- and long-term interest
rates, as well as lingering spare capacity, will
be presented as offsets that means the MPC
retains confidence that inflation will return
towards 2% over time.
• We still think a more measured tightening
approach is appropriate from here. Raising the
OCR at every meeting until something ‘breaks’
could be a risky strategy if downside growth

concerns crystalise.
• Nonetheless, the expected hike will bring the
OCR to the top of the RBNZ’s neutral range
more quickly. We now think the OCR will hit
3.5% a meeting earlier in February. Thereafter
we expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, lifting the OCR to a
peak of 4%.

RBNZ decision and communication.
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. As there will likely be differences
in perspective about recent developments in energy
prices and the exchange rate, we think it is quite possible
that the decision will be reached after a formal vote,
rather than by consensus.
As the RBNZ will not be presenting new forecasts (aside

perhaps from updated short-term CPI forecasts) we will
not see an updated OCR forecast track. Rather we expect
the RBNZ will aim to leave their options open at the next
meeting in December. As in September, we expect the
RBNZ to note that conditional on the central economic

ECONOMIC BULLETIN

Westpac New Zealand Economics
outlook, the OCR may need to increase further (the
September projection had portrayed an OCR eventually
rising to 3.25%). The RBNZ will emphasise the future OCR
path is not predetermined.
In September, the MPC agreed that further tightening in
policy was likely required. The bulk of the MPC saw upside
risks to the inflation outlook while two members (Conway
and Hansen) thought the risks were balanced. Recent
developments will likely not have made those seeing

upside risks to inflation more comfortable, while the
more dovish MPC members will likely still see the growth
downside risks given continued softness in house prices
and the mounting pressure on real incomes. Indeed, all
MPC members saw some degree of downside risks to
growth which likely remain relevant. All these messages
should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC will likely continue to present their decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July, where

a higher OCR now is insurance against a much higher OCR
later should upside medium-term inflation risks arise. We
doubt the objective will be to push market interest rates
materially higher.
The exchange rate will likely have greater prominence
at this Review, as it did at the July meeting. The TWI is
just over 3% lower than previously assumed which – if
sustained – will add some inflation pressure over the
next few quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted

the RBNZ’s medium term outlook for inflation. So, the
discussion will seek to link the implications of the lower
exchange rate for short term inflation projections with the
risk this spills over into medium term inflation. We doubt
the RBNZ will be trying to actively support the exchange
rate in any meaningful way, and indeed the lower
exchange rate may be presented as something supportive
of the medium-term growth profile, reducing risks there.
Financial conditions more broadly are likely to receive

some attention. While the exchange rate is lower than
assumed, there have also been marked increases in
wholesale long-term interest rates and (to a lesser extent)
mortgage rates. The impact of higher interest rates on
managing medium term inflation risks will likely be noted.
The RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that at this
stage there is little evidence of second-round inflation
impacts coming from the Iran war and the associated rise
in energy prices. We note that most other central banks

are drawing a similar judgement at this point. Today’s CPI
might be consistent with that judgement, although core
inflation measures have increased and will likely be noted
by the more hawkish members of the MPC. We expect the
RBNZ to point to the risks that such persistent inflation
pressures might emerge at some point. But that would
depend on the duration of the oil price shock. We expect
the RBNZ to continue to have the assumption that the
shock dissipates and that energy prices and hence CPI

inflation will be appreciably lower in the medium term.

RBNZ target range
We expect the RBNZ to note the labour market remains
cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Still-weak employment growth compared to labour force

growth, weak job vacancies growth and low levels of
labour market turnover are in line with this view and could
be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge a still-solid
global growth outlook but with significant downside risks
looking forward given the Iran war and concerns around
the sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Implications for Westpac’s forecast profile
for the OCR.
The expected hike will bring the OCR to the top of the

range of the RBNZ’s neutral rate estimates more quickly.
It seems likely that the OCR will hit 3.5% a meeting earlier
now. Beyond the pause that we had previously expected
at next week’s meeting, we had expected the RBNZ would
be looking to slow the pace of OCR increases once it had
reached 3.5%. We retain the latter expectation, so this
earlier hike also implies that the pause will come a little
earlier, after two further 25bp hikes at the December

2026 and February 2027 reviews.
We continue to see further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings – taking the OCR to a peak
of 4.0% – as it’s likely that more restrictive conditions
will be required to push core inflation back sustainably
to 2%. Needless to say, the path of the OCR in 2027 will
depend on the evolution of numerous factors that remain
very uncertain, not least being developments in the
Middle East.
Arguments in favour of a hike.

• The ongoing conflict in the Middle East and related
developments in global energy markets point to upside
risk for headline inflation, at least in the near term.
Westpac’s forecasts for headline inflation are now
at 2.5% or above for most of 2027 now. Upside risks
to core inflation and inflation expectations also may
emerge the longer the oil price remains above $90-
100/bbl.

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• The exchange rate is notably weaker in recent months
as global interest rates have been rising, and the NZ
OCR is low compared to Australian and US settings.
Financial markets even envisage further interest rate
increases in Europe given the high levels of inflation
implied by their September adverse scenarios (3.2%
2027 HICP inflation vs 2.5% baseline). If the NZ OCR
doesn’t keep pace with global trends, then the

risk is for more inflation generated by a lower NZD
exchange rate.
• The ‘real’ OCR rate is relatively low – plausibly around
-1 to -2 % based on headline inflation projections.
Based on core CPI levels the real policy rate is likely
negative. An OCR in the 3.25-3.5% range would deliver
positive real rates based on core CPI forecasts.
• The economy has grown above trend in the last year
reflecting strong export sector incomes and the
lagged impact of past low OCR settings. The output

gap is likely still negative but is closing. Relying on a
prolonged negative output gap to bring core inflation
pressures down could be risky.
• The areas of economic strength in the last year are not
yet waning. Agriculture sector returns remain strong,
while global growth forecasts are being revised up as
the AI investment boom proceeds. The construction
sector is picking up, as is the manufacturing sector.
Tourism is strong, supported by an advantageous

exchange rate.
• Market pricing leans strongly towards an OCR hike at
this meeting. It may be less disruptive to go along with
market pricing, especially given the RBNZ’s forecasts
imply two further OCR increases including one by
year-end. Moving now versus six weeks later is of little
consequence from a macro view. A smaller ‘splash’ at
this meeting could be desirable given the proximity to
the election.
Arguments in favour of no change.
• For all the upward pressure on short term inflation,

there is little evidence that the medium-term inflation
outlook has shifted much. Domestic inflation in the
September quarter was 0.3ppts weaker than the RBNZ
had expected, which should be a source of comfort.
In addition, energy and exchange rate inflation
impacts on inflation occur too early for the MPC to
offset and will be what they will be. And measures of
pricing behaviour and inflation expectations in recent
consumer and business surveys seem benign given the

scale of recent energy price increases.
• Firms report pressure on margins and an inability
to set prices high enough to recoup cost increases.
Profitability is struggling which clouds the outlook.
Firms expect better times ahead – perhaps because
they think energy prices will fall – but note tougher
trading conditions now. Hiring still seems modest
albeit positive. It does not appear that hiring is yet
strong enough to reduce the high unemployment rate.

• Housing market trends look quite weak and seem
set to constrain consumer spending and residential
investment. Despite very low mortgage rates in the
last year, house sales have declined and house prices
are flat to falling. It seems unlikely we will see a change
in these weak trends soon – especially if interest
rates continue to rise. The result is we are not seeing
a ‘consumption rich’ recovery and growth is relatively
unbalanced. There could be additional risks to the

sustainability of this recovery should external demand
suddenly weaken for some reason.
How might the election play into the
decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not
be politically motivated. However, the election outcome
is close. And should it result in a Labour Party-led
coalition, it seems likely the new government would adopt
the Labour Party’s promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual

mandate. That would require the MPC to weigh the degree
of labour market slack more highly in its deliberations. As
the degree of labour market slack currently seems high,
a new mandate might justify the MPC taking more time
to return inflation to the middle of the target range (our
earlier note discusses this issue). This could figure into
the deliberations of some MPC members if the decision
between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it now is

viewed as finely balanced.
Scenarios.
Our core scenario, with perhaps around a 60%
probability, is that the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp
and signals the December meeting is live but without
committing to a markedly tighter future stance than
presented in September. Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the OCR increase to 3% and have the RBNZ more
strongly emphasise the upside risks to core and

medium-term inflation, leading to the implication that
an upward revision of the RBNZ’s forward OCR profile
is in the offing in December. The downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) is one
where the RBNZ leaves the OCR unchanged and signals
that the medium-term outlook for inflation has not
yet changed meaningfully relative to that forecast
in the September meeting, despite the higher near-

term inflation outlook. This would cause the RBNZ to
reiterate its previous forecast, i.e. one 25bp increase in
December and one more hike in 2027 sometime.

### Page 4

4
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Appendix: Key developments since the
September Monetary Policy Statement.
Activity: The economy is coming from a stronger starting
point than the RBNZ assumed in September. GDP rose
by 0.2% in the June quarter, and along with revisions to
earlier quarters, the annual growth rate lifted to 2.6%
against the RBNZ’s forecast of 2.1%. Against this, recent
high-frequency data and the resurgence in fuel prices
presents some downside risk to the RBNZ’s growth

forecasts for the second half of the year. The RBNZ’s
Nowcast estimates of growth have drifted lower in recent
weeks, and while these are not the same as the RBNZ’s
official forecasts, they may be a useful guide as to the
direction of change.
Sentiment-based activity indicators: The NZIER’s
Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion was mixed. Firms
were more optimistic about the coming months, but
recent performance has been more subdued and
continues to fall short of expectations. Hiring and

investment intentions picked up compared to last
quarter, but more firms cited a lack of demand rather
than capacity as their main constraint on growth.
Meanwhile, the monthly PMI surveys have shown ongoing
strength in manufacturing, with more modestly positive
readings for service industries.
Housing market: Housing market conditions have
remained soft. House sales have fallen a further 4%
since July, the average time to sell remains elevated,
and prices have continued to edge lower. The RBNZ had

already assumed a modest decline in prices over 2026,
and the recent performance has been broadly in line with
their assumption.
Labour market: Filled jobs have picked up in the last
two months, having been flat through the first half of this
year. That’s in line with the RBNZ’s forecast of a rise in
employment and a tick down in the unemployment rate
to 5.5% in the September quarter. The QSBO showed
some tightening in the availability of skilled workers and a

lift in hiring intentions. Net migration has been in line with
the RBNZ’s assumptions.
Inflation: Annual inflation slowed to 3.7% in the
September quarter, below the RBNZ’s forecast of
3.9%. The downside surprise was on non-tradables
inflation, while tradables inflation was in line with the
RBNZ’s forecast. Underlying inflation pressures remain
elevated, with the various measures of core inflation
close to or above the top of the RBNZ’s 1-3% target

range. Since the September MPS forecasts were finalised,
oil prices have surged higher again, with petrol prices
setting new record highs. The Dubai crude spot price
is currently around $110/bbl, compared to the RBNZ’s
assumption of an average price of around $84/bbl for the
December quarter.
Inflation expectations: The QSBO showed that firms’
expectations of costs and prices remain elevated, but
lower than they were in the June quarter. ANZ’s monthly

survey showed a small dip in inflation expectations
among both businesses and consumers, although the
survey period may have pre-dated the worst of the
latest resurgence in fuel prices (the next updates will be
released just after the OCR review).
Trade: The June quarter terms of trade were in line with
the RBNZ’s forecast. Prices for New Zealand’s commodity
exports have generally remained firm in recent months,
with a pickup in dairy prices against some easing in beef

and lamb prices.
Financial conditions: Domestic financial conditions have
provided mixed signals since the September MPS. Long-
term interest rates have risen sharply along with global
markets, which the RBNZ may regard as an unwarranted
tightening of financial conditions to the extent that it
hasn’t reflected domestic economic conditions. Most
fixed-term mortgage rates have risen by 5-15 basis points
since the September MPS, but rates for the popular

two-year term have actually fallen a little as lenders
have looked to compete in this space. Meanwhile, the
New Zealand dollar has fallen sharply and the trade-
weighted index is just over 3% below the RBNZ’s assumed
average for the December quarter. If sustained, this will
add to imported inflation pressures over the year ahead.

Document 354: Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#china #asia #aud
Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
16:03 October 19 2026 Share
Stimulus is required if success in 2026 is to be sustained through 2027 and 2028.
China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3 2026, quarterly growth accelerating from 0.9% in Q2 to 1.2%
in Q3. Annual growth lifted from 4.3% to 4.5% as a result; though, on a year-to-date basis, growth
edged down 0.1ppts to 4.6%. The Q3 result keeps authorities on track to achieve their 4.5-5.0%ytd

ambition for 2026, and also provides a little more time to accelerate domestic demand into 2027.

### Page 2

Broad-based stimulus is required because net exports’ contribution to growth will likely abate in coming
quarters now that the trade surplus is back at record highs, and as many Chinese firms are
increasingly splitting their focus between expanding domestic capacity and offshore production
networks.

While annual retail sales growth accelerated from 0.4% to 1.1% in September, the Q3 year-to-date
growth pulse of 1.1% is just a quarter of the gain reported a year ago. Automobiles, construction
materials and jewellery have been particular heavy detractors over the past year, but most of the other
sub-categories also lack momentum. As we detailed in our September Market Outlook, this is not
because households are cash constrained, but rather as sentiment is extremely weak and the outlook
for employment and wealth highly uncertain. Key for wealth is that house prices for new and
established homes continue to decline month-on-month (respectively 0.1% and 0.3% in September)
five years into the downturn. Property investment’s ongoing decline (-20.6%ytd) suggests this
persistent weakness in sales and prices is demand driven.

While total fixed asset investment has been an outright negative for annual GDP growth since April, a
recovery is not beyond either the public or private sector’s capacity. Rather, across the economy,
entities are waiting for direction and incentive from the Central Government. This is likely to come by
early-2027 to offset the loss of support from trade, and as downside risks to global growth become
more apparent. Helpfully, industrial production continues to grow at a robust pace, 5.3%ytd, borrowing
rates for businesses and households are low, and authorities are beginning to provide banks with
additional lending capacity. For larger businesses, anecdotes suggest officials are also encouraging
major institutional investors to increase their structural allocation to domestic equities, building lasting 
appetite for new equity issuance, which in turn will fund future waves of real economic capacity and
income generation.

We remain comfortable with the view that Chinese GDP growth will hold at or slightly above 4.5%
through 2026–2028. But, the longer the Central Government waits to trigger new real investment
across both the private and public sectors, the higher the likelihood of poor sentiment becoming
entrenched and downside risks crystallising. If authorities wait until after net exports’ contribution has
faded, even holding annual aggregate growth above 4.0% could become a significant challenge. For
this reason, we expect material stimulus initiatives to be announced in the next 3 to 6 months to reset
the property sector and turn the business investment trend.

Document 470: インドネシア、財務相に続いて外相も交代、実務重視への軌道修正か ～実務家の登用で政策への信頼は回復するか、焦点は大統領主導の政権運営～

Source PDF: all report pdf/インドネシア、財務相に続いて外相も交代、実務重視への軌道修正か ～実務家の登用で政策への信頼は回復するか、焦点は大統領主導の政権運営～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 13 日

1/3 
インドネシア、財務相に続いて外相も交代、実務重視への軌道修正か 
～実務家の登用で政策への信頼は回復するか、焦点は大統領主導の政権運営～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ インドネシアのプラボウォ大統領は、 ９月の財務相交代に続いて、10 月１日に行った内閣改
造で外相などを交代させ、主要政策を担う体制を再編した。大統領の側近であるスギオノ前
外相は閣内に残っており、今回の人事を一律に側近排除や政権の求心力低下と捉えることに
は慎重さが必要である。政治的な信頼関係を維持しつつ、実務家の登用によって政策遂行体
制を見直したと解釈することもできる。 
○ 新財務相のスアハシル氏には、財政運営への信認回復や政府内の調整改善が期待される。た
だし、2027 年度予算では財政赤字をＧＤＰ比2.4％とする一方、拡張的な財政運営を維持す
る方針であり、今回の交代が緊縮財政への転換を意味するわけではない。経済成長重視の政
策を支えながら、歳入不足や追加支出に対応し、財政の持続性を確保できるかが問われる。 
○ 職業外交官出身のナシル新外相には、大統領主導の外交を支える専門的な助言や政策調整が
期待される。外交路線の大幅な変更よりも、主要国との経済協力、自国の権益、パレスチナ
支援、国内世論など、複数の外交目標の整合性を確保することが課題となる。 
○ 実務家の登用は、政策の予見可能性を高め、企業や投資家の不安を和らげる可能性がある。
一方、相次ぐ重要閣僚の交代は、政策の継続性や新閣僚の裁量への懸念を招き得る。人事の
成否は、大統領が専門的な判断をどこまで尊重し、予算執行や政府内の調整、外交上の合意
形成に反映させるかに左右される。

【財務相に続いて外相も交代、主要政策を担う体制を相次いで再編】 
インドネシアのプラボウォ大統領は 10 月１日、内閣改造を実施し、スギオノ外相の後任にアル
マナタ・ナシル外務副大臣を任命した。スギオノ氏は人間開発・文化担当調整相に就任し ており、
引き続き閣内にとどまることとなった。今回の改造では、工業相や政治・安全保障担当調整相など
も交代しており、外交にとどまらず、幅広い政策分野で政権の体制が再編された格好である。 
同国では９月 14 日にも、プルバヤ財務相が更迭され、後任にスアハシル・ナザラ財務副大臣が

### Page 2

2/3 
任命された（注１）。プルバヤ氏を巡っては、2025 年９月にスリ＝ムルヤニ氏の後任として財務相
に就任したものの、 約１年で事実上更迭されるなど交代を余儀なくされた。 プラボウォ政権の発足
から２年足らずの間に財務相が２度交代したことに加え、今回は外交を担う閣僚も交代 しており、
主要政策の責任者を相次いで入れ替える動きとなっている。 
今回の人事については、一律に「側近の排除」や「政権の求心力低下」と捉えることには慎重さ
が必要であろう。事実、大統領の側近のひとりであるスギオノ氏は閣内に残る一方、新外相には外
交官出身者が起用されている。 政治的な信頼関係を維持しつつ、 政策実務を担う人材の配置を見直
したと解釈することも可能である。このため、今回は、人事の変更そのものよりも、それが政策立
案や政府内の調整にどのような変化をもたらすかに注意する必要がある。

【財務相交代には信認回復が期待されるものの、財政拡張路線の転換とは限らない】 
財務相交代を巡っては、 財政運営の信頼性を回復できるかが焦点となっている。 プルバヤ氏の財
務相在任中は、プラボウォ大統領が志向する経済成長を重視した政策運営が進められる一方、 財政
支出の拡大や政策の予見可能性を巡る懸念が強まった。今回の交代劇の背後では、財務省内での大
規模な人事異動のほか、政府系ファンドとの調整を巡る問題も指摘されており、 財政方針だけでな
く、省内外との意思疎通も課題となっていたとされる。 
後任の財務相となったスアハシル氏は、2019 年から財務副大臣を務めてきた実務家であるため、
予算編成や財政運営への理解の深さが期待される。 就任後には、 国家財政の健全性と信頼性を維持
するとともに、予算を成長促進と経済安定に役立てる姿勢を示した。このため、同氏の登用は、成
長重視の政策を全面的に見直すというよりも、 財政への信認を維持しながら政策を実行する体制へ
の修正と捉えられる。 
事実、９月29 日に成立した2027 年度予算においては、財政赤字をＧＤＰ比2.4％とする計画が
示される一方、政府は引き続き拡張的な財政運営を維持する方針を掲げている。したがって、新財
務相の就任を、そのまま緊縮財政への転換とみなすことはできない。今後は、歳入が計画を下回る
場合や追加的な支出需要が生じた場合、 歳出の優先順位を見直すことにより、 財政の持続性を確保
することができるかが問われることになる。

【外相には職業外交官を起用、大統領主導の外交を支える調整機能が焦点】 
一方で、外相交代で注目されるのは、職業外交官が外交当局のトップに就いたことである。ナシ
ル氏は 1997 年に外務省に入省した後、外務省報道官、駐フランス大使、国連常駐代表などを歴任
し、2024 年 10 月から外務副大臣を務めてきた。二国間関係と多国間外交の双方で経験を有する同
氏の起用は、外交実務や政府内の政策調整を強化する契機となる可能性がある。 
プラボウォ政権の外交を巡っては、 大統領自身の役割が強まる一方、 外務省の関与が相対的に弱

注１ ９月15 日付レポート「インドネシア･プルバヤ財務相､就任１年で更迭」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/660239.html）

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
まっていたと指摘されている。このため、新外相には、外交上の合意をより慎重に精査し、大統領
に対して専門的な助言を行う役割が期待されているとされる。一方で、外相個人の外交経験だけで
なく、大統領による首脳外交と、外務省による実務・法的な検討をどのように結び付けるかが今後
の外交戦略の課題になる。 
こうした課題の一例として、2024 年 11 月の中国との共同声明を巡る問題が挙げられる。同声明
においては、 両国の主張が重なる海域での共同開発に言及したことから、 中国が南シナ海で主張す
る海洋権益をインドネシアが容認したのではないかとの懸念を招いた。 その後、 インドネシア外務
省は、中国の主張を認めるものではなく、従来の立場に変更はないと説明した。経済協力を進める
ための首脳間の合意が、 自国の権益に関する立場と矛盾すると受け止められないよう、 事前の調整
と対外説明を徹底する必要性を示した事例と捉えられる。 
さらに、2026 年にトランプ米政権が主導する形で発足した 「平和評議会」 への参加について、 そ
の後にイスラエルと米国がイランに対する軍事活動を開始したことを受けて、国内のイスラム団体
などから参加の見直しを求める声が上がった。外交政策には、主要国との関係構築だけでなく、パ
レスチナ支援など従来の方針や国内世論との整合性も求められる。 新外相に期待されるのは、 特定
の大国への接近や距離の取り直しを直ちに進めることよりも、 複数の外交目標を整理しつつ、 その
一貫性を確保することが求められる。

【実務家登用の成否は、大統領が専門的な判断を政策に反映させるかに左右される】 
とはいえ、新外相の起用が外交路線の大幅な変更を意味するわけではない。ナシル氏自身は、前
任者の取り組みを継承するとともに、従来の「自主・積極外交」を維持する姿勢を示している。ま
た、外交政策は大統領の権限に属しており、その指示に従うことも強調している。多国間主義を重
視し、2029～2030 年の国連安全保障理事会（安保理）非常任理事国への立候補を進める方針も、 従
来の取り組みを引き継ぐものと考えられる。 
この点では、財務相と外相の交代に共通する課題が浮かび上がる。新財務相には、政権の政策目
標を支えつつ、財政の信頼性を維持することが求められる。一方で、新外相には、大統領の外交方
針を踏まえながら、政策の一貫性を確保することが求められる。 両者はともに十分な実務経験を有
しているものの、 専門的な助言が政策決定に反映されなければ、 人事による改善効果は限定的とな
ることは避けられない。 
経済に対する影響についても、 相反する二つの面から評価する必要がある。 実務経験の豊富な人
材の登用によって、 政策説明や政府内の調整が改善すれば、 企業や投資家の不安を和らげる可能性
がある。一方で、短期間で重要閣僚の交代が繰り返されれば、政策の継続性や新閣僚の裁量に対す
る疑問が残ることで、投資判断を慎重化させる要因にもなり得ることに注意が必要である。 
したがって、今後の評価では、新閣僚の発言だけでなく、予算執行や政府内 における政策調整、
外交上の合意形成において実際の変化が生じるかを確認する必要がある。財務相と外相の交代は 、
政策運営を立て直すきっかけとなることが期待されるものの、 その成否を左右するのは、 実務家を
登用した大統領自身が、 専門的な判断をどこまで尊重するかを注視する必要がある。   以 上

派信号或推升美债收益率。
10 ⽉ 29 ⽇，美国三季度实际 GDP 初值、美联储⾸选通胀指标 PCE 指数也将出炉。 GDP 增速若超
预期或削弱降息押注，但市场更关注内核 —— 消费韧性与企业投资⾛势。若 PCE 超预期或强化
“ 更⾼更久 ” 收紧预期，直接压制⻛险资产估值。

⽇本央⾏、欧央⾏、加拿⼤央⾏、新西兰央⾏等公布利率决议
⽇本央⾏10 ⽉ 30 ⽇公布利率决议，彭博预期维持 1.25% 不变但释放政策正常化信号；彭博预期
12 ⽉再加息 25bp ⾄ 2% 终点。在⽇元疲弱与套息交易庞⼤背景下，其表态牵动全球资⾦流向。

### Page 5

欧洲央⾏10 ⽉ 29 ⽇公布利率决议，据路透调查，市场预计主要再融资利率维持 2.65% 不变；拉
加德发布会指引 12 ⽉路径。加拿⼤央⾏10 ⽉ 28 ⽇议息，彭博调查预期加息 25bp ⾄ 3.00% ；⾏⻓
Macklem 强调抗通胀未尽。作为全球央⾏超级周⼀员，其紧缩外溢值得关注。新西兰央⾏等也
将公布利率决议。此外，⽇本总务省 10 ⽉ 30 ⽇公布 10 ⽉东京 CPI，作为⽇本央⾏决议前最后关
键通胀读数，若超预期将强化⽇银政策正常化叙事、搅动套息交易。
中国制造业 PMI 与规模以上⼯业企业利润同⽐
国家统计局 10 ⽉ 31 ⽇公布 10 ⽉官⽅制造业 PMI ，前值 50.1 。作为⽉度经济景⽓⾸读数，市场关
注能否稳于荣枯线之上；此前 9 ⽉规上⼯业企业利润同⽐增 4.2% ，国庆后开⼯与订单修复情况为
短期看点。
财报
美股财报：⾕歌、微软、亚⻢逊、闪迪等；⽇韩：三星、 SK  海⼒⼠、铠侠
美股与东亚科技财报密集：⾕歌、微软于 10 ⽉ 29 ⽇盘后，英特尔、亚⻢逊 10 ⽉ 30 ⽇盘后披露；

韩国三星、 SK 海⼒⼠、⽇本铠侠同期发布。市场聚焦云与 AI 资本开⽀指引、 HBM 与存储供需，
以及 AI 硬件链定价 —— 微软、⾕歌的资本开⽀表态被视为全球 AI 算⼒景⽓的⻛向标。
A 股财报：中际旭创、天孚通信、新易盛、宇树科技，贵州茅台
A 股三季报披露进⼊尾声，中际旭创、天孚通信、新易盛、宇树科技，贵州茅台等密集亮相。作
为光模块（ CPO ）⻰头，中际旭创、新易盛、天孚通信的业绩与 1.6T 放量节奏是 AI 算⼒链核⼼
验证；宇树科技作为机器⼈新锐，⾸份公开财报亦受关注；茅台则关乎消费韧性。
三季报可能是 10 ⽉最后⼀两周最⼤的不确定性来源，并且美国微软等科技巨头也要披露最新财
报。
港股财报：⽐亚迪、中⾏、建⾏、农⾏等
港股财报季同步推进，⽐亚迪与中国⽯油 / 中国⽯化 / 中国海洋⽯油及内银⻰头（中⾏ / 建⾏ / 农⾏ /
邮储）陆续放榜；在南向资⾦持续流⼊与利率⻅顶预期下，其指引影响港股⾼股息与能源主
线。

员。  在以⾊列仍⾯临多线作战压⼒、美以关系因伊朗问题上的分歧跌⼊低⾕、其本⼈司法问题
缠⾝的情况下，现任以⾊列总理内塔尼亚胡能否继续执政，成为这次⼤选中的主要悬念。当地
时间 14 ⽇，央视新闻记者获悉，以⾊列总理内塔尼亚胡当天表⽰，在即将到来的以⾊列议会选
举举⾏前，他反对美国或以⾊列在未来⼏天内对伊朗发动军事袭击，并称在现阶段采取此类⾏
动 “ 并不⽅便 ” 。

有候选⼈均未获得超过 50% 的有效选票，得票前两名的候选⼈进⼊第⼆轮对决。  初步计票结果
预计将在第⼆轮投票结束后的数⼩时内（当地时间当晚 / 北京时间次⽇凌晨）陆续揭晓。专家认
为，此次巴西总统选举将决定巴西未来 4 年⾛向，并可能牵动拉美政治格局。

国共产党第⼆⼗届中央委员会第五次全体会议于 10 ⽉ 26 ⽇⾄ 29 ⽇在北京召开。

蘇子錚 
Alex.Su@yuanta.com 
劉東萌 
miketmliu@yuanta.com

伺服器大爆發，營收大幅優於預期 
和碩 3Q26 營收 3,890 億元，季增 42%、年增 51%，主要受惠於美系客戶
的 GB300 產品於 9 月開始拉貨，且不只原先的 NeoCloud 業者，大型業者
也開始拉貨，帶動伺服器營收大幅度成長；預估毛利率 4.1%，季減 0.3 個
百分點、年減 0.1 個百分點，主因低毛利率的消費性產品營收比重提升，且
受到立訊股價大跌影響，預計業外虧損達 6.9 億元，主要來自於金融資產評
價減損，EPS 1.22 元，上修 15%。 
GB300 伺服器訂單將持續至 4Q26  
展望 4Q26，我們認為手機部分，iPhone18 產品開始出貨，低階版本也開始
小量產，筆電部分，記憶體價格仍上揚，且 筆電價格提升影響終端購買需求，
預期出貨量將再次下降，且因成本提升，加工費未成長，毛利率承壓，伺服
器部分，美系 NeoCloud 及大型業者持續對 GB300 產品拉貨，並預期 9 月
訂單強度將持續。我們認為，儘管消費性產品 4Q26 營收恐衰退，手機營收
恐旺季不旺，但在伺服器客戶對於 GB300 需求仍強勁下，營收仍維持成長，
預估 4Q26 營收 4,212 億元，季增 8%，EPS 2.45 元，季增 101%。 
靜待明年 VR200 持續帶動營運成長 
儘管消費性市場仍未起色，但伺服器市場需求仍持續強勁成長，和碩自身的
伺服器產品開始出貨，且明年可望取得來自美系大廠 VR200 訂單，帶動伺
服器業務大幅成長；另外，永擎及景碩兩子公司營收及獲利持續強勁成長，
整體產品組合明顯改善。我們正向看待和碩未來伺服器及其子公司相關伺服
器產品的成長動能，上修 2026/27 年獲利 23%/24%，上調評等至買進，目
標價上修至 116 元以 2027 年預估每股盈餘及 13 倍本益比推得。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 營收優於預期 33%，主因伺服器業務於 9 月開始放量，主要為
GB300 高單價產品。 
⧫  
GB300 訂單將維持強勁態勢至 4Q26，並靜待明年 VR200 產品拉貨，
伺服器業務將迎來大成長。 
⧫  
伺服器業務迎爆發期，子公司獲利持續提升，上修 2026/27 年獲利
23%/24%，上調持有至買進，目標價上修至 116 元。

### Page 2

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：ODM/EMS 代工 2026 年 10 月 12 日 Pegatron 第2 頁，共 17頁  
營運分析  
伺服器大爆發，營收大幅優於預期 
管理層原先預期 3Q26，保守看待筆電市場，手機部分僅 18 Pro 可量產，通訊業務壓力較大，伺服
器需求強勁，伺服器營收貢獻將持續成長。整體而言， 3Q26 符合管理層的預期，但手機及伺服器
業務成長幅度仍大幅度優於管理層預期，尤其是美系大型業者拉貨時程略提前。  
和碩 3Q26 營收 3,890 億元，季增 42%、年增 51%，主要受惠於美系客戶的 GB300 產品於 9 月開
始拉貨，且不只原先的 NeoCloud 業者，大型業者也開始拉貨，帶動伺服器營收大幅度成長；預估
毛利率 4.1%，季減 0.3 個百分點、年減 0.1 個百分點，主因低毛利率的消費性產品營收比重提升，
且受到立訊股價大跌影響，預計業外 虧損達 6.9 億元，主要來自於金融資產評價減損， EPS 1.22
元，上修 15%。 
展望 4Q26，我們認為手機部分，iPhone18 產品開始出貨，低階版本也開始小量產，筆電部分，記
憶體價格仍上揚，且筆電價格提升影響終端購買需求，預期出貨量將再次下降，且因成本提升，加
工費未成長，毛利率承壓，伺服器部分，美系 NeoCloud 及大型業者持續對 GB300 產品拉貨，並
預期 9 月訂單強度將持續。我們認為，儘管消費性產品 4Q26 營收恐衰退，手機營收恐旺季不旺，
但在伺服器客戶對於 GB300 需求仍強勁下，營收仍維持成長，預估 4Q26 營收 4,212 億元，季增
8%，EPS 2.45 元，季增 101%。

伺服器業務今明年將迎大成長 
和碩的伺服器客戶主要是 CSP 業者及 Neocloud ( 新興雲端業者)，受惠於今明年大小型業者都將開
始大量採購伺服器，不論是 HGX、GB、VR 伺服器都有機會出貨。公司目前為應對 GB300 強勁需
求，預計美國德州廠 3 月底開始就會加入生產行列，且 VR200 產品目前已送樣，可望通過客戶認
證，並在 2027 年開始量產。 
和碩的美系 NeoCloud 及大型業者持續對 GB300 產品拉貨，並預期 9 月訂單強度將持續，10-11
月的單月 GB300 量將小幅優於 9 月。且明年可望取得來自美系大廠 VR200 訂單，帶動伺服器業務
大幅成長；另外，永擎及景碩兩子公司營收及獲利持續強勁成長，整體產品組合明顯改善。

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：ODM/EMS 代工 2026 年 10 月 12 日 Pegatron 第4 頁，共 17頁  
產業概況  
AI、ASIC、通用型強勢出擊，2026 年美系 CSP 資本支出估年增 96%  
在 ChatGPT 等 AI 應用陸續推向市場，各大業者紛紛開始研發自有超級大模型，包括 Gemini 
(Google)、Claude (Anthropic) 、Llama (Meta) 等，各大品牌開始大舉投入大型語言模型(LLM)市場，
且每一個產品的智慧皆持續提高，部分消費者也陸續從 ChatGPT 陣營轉移至 Gemini 陣營，反映
出消費者開始逐漸提升對 AI 的黏著度。根據 Grand View Research 調查，2024 年全球 AI 市場營
收約 2,792 億美元，預估 2030 年市場規模超過 3 兆美元，擁有超過 30% 的年複合增長率。 
面對這爆發性的長期成長趨勢，CSP 業者(Amazon、Microsoft、Google、Meta)在法說會上一致強
調，AI 投資已轉變為一項橫跨 2026 年的結構性戰略，CSP 大廠不僅持續擴大對 GB300 等次世代
AI 伺服器的採購，更將重點放在資料中心基礎設施的長期戰略規劃，以應對 AI 運算對電力及散熱
系統強勁成長的需求，確保未來數年內擁有足夠的能源與容量來支持持續迭代的大模型部署。本中
心認為，2026 年美國主要 CSP 資本支出將年增 96%，且將有一半的成長來自於資料中心建置，代
表了資料中心的不足及未來伺服器需求。明年不僅僅是 NVIDIA GB300 的天下，AWS、Google、
Meta ASIC 的崛起也將是一大關鍵，其中又以 Google TPU 需求最為強勁，已經有兩個客戶預計將
採購 TPU。 
另外，受惠於 AI 訓練與推論需求爆發的連帶效應，本中心預估 2026 年通用型伺服器出貨量將年增
23%，其中以存儲型伺服器需求最為強勁。隨著生成式 AI 應用普及和使用者數量暴增，CSP 等業
者必須不斷擴充容量，且不僅限於高階 GPU 伺服器，龐大的 AI 模型訓練和推論在使用狀態中將消
耗大量的數據(DATA)，使得存儲型伺服器的需求大增。

### Page 5

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：ODM/EMS 代工 2026 年 10 月 12 日 Pegatron 第5 頁，共 17頁  
ASIC 進入高速成長期，GPU 需求仍維持擴張 
從 AI 晶片預期出貨來看，GPU 與 ASIC 需求均持續成長，但 2026 年後 ASIC 成長動能明顯加速。
Nvidia GPU 出貨量預估由 2025 年 565.7 萬顆增至 2026 年 650 萬顆、2027 年 850 萬顆，年增
17%及 34%；AMD 亦由 2025 年 90 萬顆增至 2027 年 180 萬顆，顯示通用 GPU 算力需求仍維持
成長。 
相較之下，ASIC 受惠 CSP 加速自研晶片及導入規模擴大，總出貨量預估由 2025 年 380 萬顆大幅
增至 2026 年 725 萬顆、2027 年 1,360 萬顆，年增率達 91%及 88%，成長速度明顯高於 GPU。其
中 Google 仍為最大 ASIC 使用者， 2027 年用量預估達 800 萬顆，AWS 亦增至 320 萬顆，
Microsoft 及 Meta 同步擴大部署。我們認為，在資料中心電力逐漸成為算力擴張瓶頸下， CSP 將
更重視每瓦運算效能及整體運算成本， ASIC 針對特定運算工作的效率優勢有望推升滲透率，惟
GPU 憑藉通用性及軟體生態系優勢，仍將維持重要地位。

### Page 6

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：ODM/EMS 代工 2026 年 10 月 12 日 Pegatron 第6 頁，共 17頁  
各大模型每週文字請求持續增加，AI 伺服器需求將持續提升 
根據 OpenRouter 統計，2025 年 9 月至 2026 年 8 月底，平台內 AI 模型每週文字請求次數由約 8
億次增加至逾 50 億次，年增超過 5 倍，顯示生成式 AI 的實際使用需求仍處於快速成長階段。
OpenRouter 主要整合 OpenAI、Google、Anthropic、DeepSeek 及 Qwen 等不同業者的模型，讓
使用者可透過單一平台選擇及呼叫多種大型語言模型，雖然 OpenRouter 僅為全球眾多 AI 服務平
台之一，但其涵蓋的模型開發商廣泛，可作為觀察 AI 應用活躍度及市場需求變化的重要參考。 AI
需求並未因前期高速成長而趨緩，反而隨著模型能力提升、使用成本下降及應用範圍擴大，持續帶
動平台請求次數創高。 
過去 AI 應用多以簡單問答為主，使用者輸入一個問題，再由模型產生一次回答；隨著 AI Agent 逐
步普及，模型開始具備規劃任務、讀取文件、搜尋資料、呼叫工具及執行程式等能力，部分任務還
會同時啟動多個子任務，再由主模型彙整結果，即使使用者表面上只提出一次要求，背後實際執行
的模型呼叫次數及 Token 消耗量都可能較傳統聊天應用增加數倍。因此，未來 AI 運算需求將同時
受到請求次數增加及單次請求 Token 用量提升所推動，實際 Token 需求增幅可能高於圖表所呈現
的請求次數成長。 
在模型使用頻率與單次運算量同步提高的情況下，全球雲端服務商及大型科技公司仍須持續擴充 AI
伺服器基礎建設。NVIDIA GPU 具備較高的通用性及成熟的軟體生態，能夠較快支援持續更新的模
型架構，適合模型種類多元及快速迭代的使用環境；Google TPU 等 ASIC 則是針對特定 AI 運算設
計的客製化晶片，當模型架構、運算模式及流量相對穩定，且 Token 規模足以分攤前期軟體最佳
化成本時，通常有機會提供較低的每 Token 成本與功耗。由於未來 AI 市場將同時存在快速更新的
前沿模型、固定的大規模推論服務及不同企業的客製化應用，GPU 與 ASIC 預期將各自對應不同工
作負載，而非由單一架構完全取代另一方。整體 Token 需求持續上升，將推動兩類 AI 伺服器建置
同步擴張，並進一步帶動運算晶片、記憶體、高速傳輸、散熱及電源等相關零組件需求。

圖 6：OpenRouter 平台內 AI 模型每週文字請求次數

### Page 7

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：ODM/EMS 代工 2026 年 10 月 12 日 Pegatron 第7 頁，共 17頁  
各 AI 運算需求持續攀升，CSP 與 NeoCloud 加速擴建 
Amazon、Google、Meta 及 Microsoft 主要 CSP 目前營運中的 AI 資料中心電力容量合計約
15.18GW，另有 15.71GW 正在建置、及已宣布的 16.08GW，若相關計畫如期落地，整體電力容
量可望由目前的 15.18GW 擴大至約 46.97GW，約為現有規模的 3.1 倍。除大型 CSP 持續投入外，
CoreWeave、Crusoe、Nebius 及 IREN 等 NeoCloud 業者也積極擴充 AI 基礎設施。NeoCloud 是
以 GPU 運算及 AI 雲端服務為主要業務的新型雲端業者，目前營運中的電力容量合計約 7.01GW，
加計在建及已宣布項目後，總規模可達約 16.01GW，約為現有規模的 2.3 倍。 
從業者的電力容量規劃來看，AI 基礎設施投資仍處於快速擴張階段。4 家主要 CSP 在建及已宣布
容量合計達 31.79GW，高於目前已營運容量的 2 倍；NeoCloud 業者後續新增容量也達 9.01GW，
顯示 AI 運算需求不僅集中於既有大型雲端服務商，新型 AI 雲端業者也正加快建置速度。隨著生成
式 AI 使用量持續增加，以及 AI Agent 帶動單次請求的 Token 消耗、推理步驟與模型呼叫次數提升，
資料中心需要配置更多 AI 伺服器以支應新增運算需求。 
各業者最終採用的伺服器架構可能因工作負載而異。模型種類多、更新速度快的環境，通常較適合
採用 NVIDIA GPU 伺服器；模型架構與運算模式相對固定，且使用規模足夠大的情況下， Google 
TPU 等 ASIC 伺服器則有機會提供較低的每 Token 成本。無論最終採用 GPU 或 ASIC，電力容量
持續擴張均代表 AI 伺服器部署量仍有明顯成長空間，並將同步帶動運算晶片、記憶體、高速傳輸、
電源及散熱等相關供應鏈需求。

獲利調整與股票評價

和碩持續提升車用及伺服器營收比重，已成為 Nvidia 合作夥伴，可望布局未來 MGX 伺服器產品，
並持續推進自家水冷設計技術，GB300 於 3Q25 出貨給客戶認證，預估和碩的伺服器營收爆發成
長，並有機會成長 10 倍以上。 
儘管消費性市場仍未起色，但伺服器市場需求仍持續強勁成長，和碩自身的伺服器產品開始出貨，
且明年可望取得來自美系大廠 VR200 訂單，帶動伺服器業務大幅成長；另外，永擎及景碩兩子公
司營收及獲利持續強勁成長，整體產品組合明顯改善。我們正向看待和碩未來伺服器及其子公司相
關伺服器產品的成長動能，上修 2026/27 年獲利 23%/24%，上調評等至買進，目標價上修至 116
元以 2027 年預估每股盈餘及 13 倍本益比推得。

公司簡介  
和碩成立於 2007 年，業務包含開發電腦周邊產品、通信技術及消費性電子產品予品牌供應商，從事產品組件的開發、設計
和製造，結合 EMS 與 ODM 產業，成為新興 DMS 企業(設計整合服務製造)。主要產品包括手機、筆記型電腦、桌上型電腦、
顯示器及車用電子等，營業據點遍布全球，大陸生產廠區包括蘇州、重慶、昆山、上海共四個廠區，台灣則有桃園、新店
廠，另有捷克、墨西哥、印尼等廠，此外，北美(墨西哥廠)、印度及越南三座新廠區於 2022 年開始量產。 
隨美系手機組裝業務利潤率逐漸降低，和碩近年來積極投入 IoT、AI、5G 等相關技術研發與產品設計進行轉型，相關產品
應用裝置技術皆有所提升。和碩長期耕耘的車用電子，也切入美系電動車大廠供應鏈，包括高階 ECU ( 電子控制元件)、車
用平板、機構件、線束、通訊模組等陸續發酵，目前對營收的貢獻仍小，隨著歐洲、美洲、亞洲客戶需求穩定成長，預估
未來 5-10 年車用電子產品將保持每年雙位數的成長，成長可期。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 和碩聯合整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於中風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於略微領
先的位置，在科技硬體行業的公司中排名同樣略微領先同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險 ： 和碩聯合的整體曝險屬於中等水準，但略高於科技硬體行業的平均水準。公司較需要改善的  
ESG 議題包含公司數據安全、人力資源、商業道德等。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：和碩聯合在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬中等。公司清楚揭露 ESG 相
關事宜，且積極面對環境保護等議題和確實實施永續供應鏈，例如禁用童工等，表明對投資者和社會的強烈責任感。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

会议指出，⼈⼝是经济社会发展的基础条件，要更好统筹⼈⼝与经济社会、资源环境的关
系，进⼀步完善相关政策，持续积累和释放⼈⼝综合红利，以⼈⼝⾼质量发展更好⽀撑经

摘要：
国务院总理李强 10 ⽉ 23 ⽇主持召开国务院常务会议，研究统筹⼈⼝与经济社会发展有关⼯作，
听取推进全国服务业⼤会部署落实情况汇报。
会议指出，⼈⼝是经济社会发展的基础条件，要更好统筹⼈⼝与经济社会、资源环境的关系，
进⼀步完善相关政策，持续积累和释放⼈⼝综合红利，以⼈⼝⾼质量发展更好⽀撑经济社会⾼
质量发展。要更加突出⼈的全⾯发展，提⾼⼈⼝科学⽂化、职业技能、⾝⼼健康等⽔平，加快

完善全⺠终⾝学习机制，⼴泛开展技能培训，强化教育、培训与产业发展协同联动，促进⾼质
量充分就业。要加强区域⼈⼝发展分类指导，引导城乡⼈⼝有序流动，稳步推进基本公共服务
均等化，根据⼈⼝分布情况优化基础设施布局，推动公共服务资源与常住⼈⼝变化动态适配。
要加快建设⽣育友好型社会，持续完善婚嫁、⽣育、养育、教育等政策，优化⾯向家庭的财
税、社保、住房等⽀持举措，努⼒稳定新出⽣⼈⼝规模。
会议指出，要进⼀步发挥服务业对经济发展的⽀撑作⽤，加⼒推进服务业扩能提质，把服务业
潜⼒空间转化为发展动能。要加快破解服务业发展的难点堵点，聚焦⽣产⽣活重点领域因业施
策，加强融资、⽤地、⼈才、数据等⽀持，稳步推进公共事业领域价格改⾰，加⼤⼒度盘活⽤
好闲置资源。要按照建设全国统⼀⼤市场的要求，持续破除市场准⼊障碍，清理⺠间投资隐性
壁垒，加快构建与新业态发展相适应的监管⽅式。要充分调动各⽅⾯发展服务业的积极性，加
强分类指导和督促协调，增强政策针对性有效性，⽀持有条件的地⽅先⾏先试，因地制宜探索
服务业创新发展路径。要⿎励扩⼤服务业投资，释放服务消费潜⼒，培育⻰头企业和特⾊品

牌。要进⼀步健全适应服务业发展的统计监测体系，强化上下联动、⻬抓共管的⼯作格局。
会议还研究了其他事项。
